From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.9, F33.9, F34.1, F43.20\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, I’m glad you came in today. What brings you in?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess what brings me in is... I just don't have the push I used to have. I used to be involved in things, you know, like choir, and my quilting, or going out to visit folks. But lately, most days I just sit at home. I keep the TV on low and just sit there, even though I know people are expecting me to show up. I just don't have it in me right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean... I'm not crying or feeling sad or anything like that. It's not like I'm down in the dumps. I just... well, I just don't have the drive anymore. I don't really feel bad in my mood, I just sit there.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy things like choir, quilting, or visiting people when you actually do them?\n\nPatient: I'm not rightly sure. I guess... well, I haven't really been doing them to find out. I just haven't had the urge to pick up my quilting or go see anybody, so I couldn't really tell you if I'd still enjoy it or not.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this lack of drive?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose... it's been building up for a while now. Ever since I retired from the school office, which was a little over two years ago. It just sort of started then and kept going.\n\nDoctor: Did this change start after your retirement?\n\nPatient: Yes, that's right. Right after I finished up at the school. Once I didn't have to go in to work anymore... well, that's when I really started noticing I just didn't want to do much of anything else either.\n\nDoctor: Does your lack of drive feel mainly like a reaction to retiring?\n\nPatient: I don't rightly know if I'd call it a reaction. I mean... I wasn't sad or upset about leaving the job or anything like that. It was just time. It's more like... well, once I stopped having to go in, my get-up-and-go just sort of went with it. I couldn't really tell you why.\n\nDoctor: For most of the past two years, have you felt down or low in yourself?\n\nPatient: No, I can't really say that I have. I don't feel sad or... low in my spirits, no. I'm not sitting around crying or feeling bad about myself. It's really just what I said before—I just don't have the urge to do much of anything. But my mood itself... well, it's mostly been fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about things, even when there isn’t a clear reason to?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I'm not really a worrier. I mean, I don't sit around fretting about things, if that's what you mean. There's not much to worry about anyway.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you felt hopeless?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. I haven't felt hopeless. I mean... I don't feel like things are bad or anything like that. I'm just taking it day by day, even if I'm not doing much of anything. So no, no hopeless feelings.\n\nDoctor: When you do make yourself do something you used to enjoy, do you still get pleasure from it?\n\nPatient: Well... I guess I'm not really sure. I haven't really tried to do much of anything lately, to be honest. So I don't know if I'd enjoy it or not. I just haven't had the energy or the mind to even try.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two years, have there been stretches of at least two weeks when you felt sad, empty, or depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had anything like that. I don't really get sad or depressed. I mean, I know I just told you I don't do much these days, but it's not because I'm sitting around crying or feeling down. So... no, I haven't had any stretches like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel tired or low on energy most days?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel tired. It's not like I need to sleep all day or anything. Like I said, I just don't have the mind to do much, but it's not because I'm physically tired or out of energy. I just... sit there mostly.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you often felt unhappy or blue, even if not intensely sad?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel blue or unhappy. I know it probably sounds strange since I just sit around so much, but... no, it's not a sad feeling at all. I just feel kind of blank, I guess. But definitely not blue.\n\nDoctor: When you actually do something you used to enjoy, do you still enjoy it?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. Like I said, I don't do much of anything these days, so... I haven't really noticed. If I do happen to do something, it's just fine, I guess. I don't really think about whether I'm enjoying it or not.\n\nDoctor: Before these past two years, was there ever a time in your life when you had at least two weeks of feeling depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I can't remember ever feeling like that. I've had normal days and bad days like anyone else, but... no, never two straight weeks of feeling depressed or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel little interest or pleasure in almost all activities most days?\n\nPatient: Well, no, I wouldn't say that. Like I mentioned before, I know I don't do a whole lot right now, but... it's not like I have zero interest in things. I just kind of keep to myself and sit quietly. I don't think I've lost all my pleasure in everything, I just... I'm just not doing much lately to really notice. So no, I don't think I feel that way.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I've had to step back from leading the choir... and I stopped helping with the projects down at the senior center. I just wasn't keeping up with what I promised folks I'd do, so I let those go.
